24	# ── Status / type value sets (TEXT + CHECK; not native PG enums for migration ergonomics) ──
25	CONNECTION_TYPES = ("snowflake", "adls_gen2", "s3", "unity_catalog", "adf", "airflow")
26	RUN_STATUSES = ("queued", "running", "succeeded", "failed", "cancelled")
27	# Result statuses. The four severity tiers (ADR 0005) are health-score-bearing —
28	# the score aggregate sums their weights over N. The two operational statuses
29	# (#122) are orthogonal: 'skip' = not evaluated, 'error' = evaluation threw
30	# (distinct from 'fail', a successful evaluation that breached). Operational
31	# statuses carry NO penalty weight and MUST be excluded from the health-score N
32	# (i.e. aggregate WHERE status IN the four tiers only).
33	_RESULT_SEVERITY_TIERS = ("pass", "warn", "fail", "critical")
34	_RESULT_OPERATIONAL_STATUSES = ("skip", "error")
35	RESULT_STATUSES = _RESULT_SEVERITY_TIERS + _RESULT_OPERATIONAL_STATUSES
36	# Monitor-kind discriminator (ADR 0012; `comparison` reserved by ADR 0014). v1
37	# only ever writes 'expectation'; the rest are constraint-valid but unused.
38	CHECK_KINDS = ("expectation", "freshness", "volume", "schema_drift", "anomaly", "comparison")
39	PIPELINE_RUN_STATUSES = ("queued", "running", "succeeded", "failed", "cancelled")
40	ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS = ("adf", "airflow")
41	PERMISSIONS = ("view", "edit", "admin")
42	ENVS = ("dev", "qa", "uat", "prod")
43	# Per-suite alert delivery threshold (suite_notifications.alert_on). 'fail' =
44	# fail/critical only, 'warn' = warn+, 'always' = every terminal run.
45	ALERT_ON_POLICIES = ("fail", "warn", "always")

48	def _uuid_pk() -> Mapped[uuid.UUID]:
49	    return mapped_column(
50	        UUID(as_uuid=True),
51	        primary_key=True,
52	        server_default=text("gen_random_uuid()"),
53	    )

56	def _created_at() -> Mapped[datetime]:
57	    return mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=False, server_default=func.now())
58	
59	
60	def _updated_at() -> Mapped[datetime]:
61	    return mapped_column(
62	        DateTime(timezone=True),
63	        nullable=False,
64	        server_default=func.now(),
65	        onupdate=func.now(),
66	    )

69	def _in_check(column: str, values: tuple[str, ...], name: str) -> CheckConstraint:
70	    quoted = ", ".join(f"'{v}'" for v in values)
71	    return CheckConstraint(f"{column} IN ({quoted})", name=name)

86	class Connection(Base):
87	    __tablename__ = "connections"
88	    __table_args__ = (
89	        _in_check("type", CONNECTION_TYPES, "type_valid"),
90	        _in_check("env", ENVS, "env_valid"),
91	        UniqueConstraint("name", "env", name="uq_connections_name_env"),
92	        # Orchestration providers (ADF, Airflow) are singletons per env: at most
93	        # one connection per (provider, env), the binding `trigger_bindings`
94	        # assumes (ADR 0004, #72). Datasources are deliberately excluded — e.g.
95	        # Snowflake DEV can have many connections (different databases) — so this
96	        # is a *partial* unique index over the orchestration types only.
97	        Index(
98	            "uq_connections_orchestrator_type_env",
99	            "type",
100	            "env",
101	            unique=True,
102	            postgresql_where=text(
103	                "type IN (" + ", ".join(f"'{p}'" for p in ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS) + ")"
104	            ),
105	        ),
106	        Index("ix_connections_created_by", "created_by"),
107	    )

109	    id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = _uuid_pk()
110	    name: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(128), nullable=False)
111	    type: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(32), nullable=False)
112	    env: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(16), nullable=False)
113	    config: Mapped[dict[str, Any]] = mapped_column(
114	        JSONB, nullable=False, server_default=text("'{}'::jsonb")
115	    )
116	    # Nullable by design (PR #41 review): holds the SecretStore key (`conn-<id>`)
117	    # once a credential is written, but is NULL for (a) the transient window
118	    # between row flush and secret write in create_connection, and (b)
119	    # credential-less auth — managed identity (ADLS) / IAM role (S3), deferred to
120	    # Week 7 (ADR 0010/0011) — plus any unauthenticated source. v1 connection types
121	    # are all secret-bearing and enforce presence in the service layer
122	    # (test_connection → 502 without a stored credential), so the column stays
123	    # NULL-able for the W7 credential-less modes without a later migration.
124	    secret_ref: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(256))
125	    created_by: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = mapped_column(
126	        UUID(as_uuid=True), ForeignKey("users.id"), nullable=False
127	    )
128	    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = _created_at()
129	    updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = _updated_at()

132	class ConnectionVersion(Base):
133	    """An immutable snapshot of a connection's editable, **non-secret** state,
134	    written on create and after every successful name/config update — the source
135	    for the connection "version history" view. Mirrors `check_versions`: per-entity
136	    config history, not the cross-entity audit log (deferred — see #310).
137	
138	    Deliberately omits the credential: the secret lives only in the SecretStore
139	    (referenced by `secret_ref`, which is the constant `conn-<id>` pointer, not the
140	    value), so it is never copied here. A credential rotation (`reauth`, or an
141	    update that only changes the secret) therefore records **no** version — that is
142	    an audit-log concern, not config history.
143	
144	    `version_no` is a per-connection sequence starting at 1 (unique with
145	    `connection_id`). A version is cascade-deleted with its connection (history is
146	    not retained past deletion — accepted), but survives its author (`changed_by`
147	    is `SET NULL`).
148	    """
149	
150	    __tablename__ = "connection_versions"
151	    __table_args__ = (
152	        UniqueConstraint("connection_id", "version_no", name="uq_connection_versions_conn_version"),
153	        Index("ix_connection_versions_connection_id", "connection_id"),
154	    )
155	
156	    id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = _uuid_pk()
157	    connection_id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = mapped_column(
158	        UUID(as_uuid=True), ForeignKey("connections.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), nullable=False
159	    )
160	    version_no: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(Integer, nullable=False)
161	    # Snapshot of the editable fields. type/env are immutable on a connection but
162	    # snapshotted for a self-contained record; `config` is the non-secret
163	    # datasource config as stored. No credential / secret_ref (see class docstring).
164	    name: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(128), nullable=False)
165	    type: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(32), nullable=False)
166	    env: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(16), nullable=False)
167	    config: Mapped[dict[str, Any]] = mapped_column(JSONB, nullable=False)
168	    # Who authored this version. NULL for a system/unknown actor or once the user
169	    # is removed — the snapshot must outlive its author (SET NULL, not CASCADE).
170	    changed_by: Mapped[uuid.UUID | None] = mapped_column(
171	        UUID(as_uuid=True), ForeignKey("users.id", ondelete="SET NULL")
172	    )
173	    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = _created_at()
174	
175	    author: Mapped["User | None"] = relationship()
176	
177	    @property
178	    def changed_by_name(self) -> str | None:
179	        """The author's display name (or email) for the history view, or None for
180	        a system actor / removed user. Reads the eager-loaded `author` — callers
181	        that serialize this must `selectinload(ConnectionVersion.author)`."""
182	        return (self.author.display_name or self.author.email) if self.author else None

199	    # Catalog 3-level name the suite's checks run against. Shaped like the column
200	    # profiler request (`table`/`schema`/`catalog`/`path`/`file_format`) and
201	    # resolved per connection type to the runner's (table, schema, catalog) by
202	    # `services.run_target.resolve_target`. NULL = targetless = not yet runnable.
203	    target: Mapped[dict[str, Any] | None] = mapped_column(JSONB)
204	    # Column-redaction policy for failing-row samples (#415): which columns may be
205	    # shown vs masked when surfacing `Result.sample_failures`. Shape:
206	    # `{"identifier_column": str, "pii_columns": [str]}` — the identifier is always
207	    # shown (so a failing row is locatable; must be non-PII) and `pii_columns` are
208	    # always masked; unclassified columns still default-redact (security can't
209	    # regress). NULL = no policy → the blanket-mask fallback. Suite-level for v1
210	    # (a suite targets one table); shaped to promote to a connection/column catalog
211	    # later. Auto-derivable from datasource classification/tags + name heuristics;
212	    # this column stores the resolved/overridden policy.
213	    column_policy: Mapped[dict[str, Any] | None] = mapped_column(JSONB)

261	class CheckVersion(Base):
262	    """An immutable snapshot of a check's editable state, written on create and
263	    after every successful update — the source for the "version history" drawer
264	    ("see previous config before overwriting"). v1 is view-only; restore is a
265	    deferred follow-up. This is per-check config history, not the cross-entity
266	    audit log (deferred to v1.1).
267	
268	    `version_no` is a per-check sequence starting at 1 (unique with `check_id`).
269	    Rows survive the check (`ondelete=SET NULL` on `changed_by` for a deleted
270	    author) but are cascade-deleted with the check itself.
271	    """
272	
273	    __tablename__ = "check_versions"
274	    __table_args__ = (
275	        UniqueConstraint("check_id", "version_no", name="uq_check_versions_check_version"),
276	        Index("ix_check_versions_check_id", "check_id"),
277	    )

282	    )
283	    version_no: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(Integer, nullable=False)
284	    # Snapshot of the editable check fields (kind is immutable but snapshotted for
285	    # a self-contained record). `config` is the GX expectation kwargs, as stored.
286	    name: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(256), nullable=False)
287	    kind: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(32), nullable=False)
288	    expectation_type: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(128), nullable=False)

291	    fail_threshold: Mapped[Decimal | None] = mapped_column(Numeric)
292	    critical_threshold: Mapped[Decimal | None] = mapped_column(Numeric)
293	    # Who authored this version. NULL for a system/unknown actor or once the user

295	    changed_by: Mapped[uuid.UUID | None] = mapped_column(

310	class Run(Base):
311	    __tablename__ = "runs"
312	    __table_args__ = (
313	        _in_check("status", RUN_STATUSES, "status_valid"),
314	        Index("ix_runs_suite_id", "suite_id"),
315	        Index("ix_runs_status", "status"),
316	        # Trigger-dedup race guard (#308): one suite run per orchestration
317	        # pipeline-run event. Partial — orchestration markers only
318	        # (`<provider>:<pipeline>:<run_id>`); manual/probe/schedule markers
319	        # legitimately repeat. Predicate mirrors the migration + the service's
320	        # ON CONFLICT (orchestration_service._ORCH_TRIGGER_PREDICATE).
321	        Index(
322	            "uq_runs_suite_triggered_by",
323	            "suite_id",
324	            "triggered_by",
325	            unique=True,
326	            postgresql_where=text("triggered_by LIKE 'adf:%' OR triggered_by LIKE 'airflow:%'"),
327	        ),
328	    )

330	    id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = _uuid_pk()
331	    # CASCADE (#540): runs (and their results, via the run_id FK) die with the
332	    # suite — ADR 0020's accepted cascade posture. Without it a suite that had
333	    # ever run 500'd on delete.
334	    suite_id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = mapped_column(
335	        UUID(as_uuid=True), ForeignKey("suites.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), nullable=False
336	    )

339	    # Celery task id of the dispatched run_suite task, captured at dispatch so a
340	    # cancel can revoke a still-queued task. NULL until dispatched (or if dispatch
341	    # failed). String(155): Celery ids are UUIDs but keep headroom.
342	    celery_task_id: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(155))
343	    started_at: Mapped[datetime | None] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True))

438	            "provider",
439	            "pipeline_or_dag_id",
440	            "env",
441	            "suite_id",
442	            name="uq_trigger_bindings_lookup",
443	        ),
444	        Index(
445	            "ix_trigger_bindings_provider_pipeline_env",
446	            "provider",
447	            "pipeline_or_dag_id",
448	            "env",
449	        ),
450	        Index("ix_trigger_bindings_suite_id", "suite_id"),
451	    )
452	
453	    id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = _uuid_pk()
454	    provider: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(16), nullable=False)
455	    pipeline_or_dag_id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(256), nullable=False)
456	    env: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(16), nullable=False)
457	    suite_id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = mapped_column(
458	        UUID(as_uuid=True), ForeignKey("suites.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), nullable=False
459	    )
460	    enabled: Mapped[bool] = mapped_column(Boolean, nullable=False, server_default=text("true"))
461	    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = _created_at()
462	    updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = _updated_at()
463	
464	
465	class Schedule(Base):
466	    """A cron schedule that fires a suite run automatically (A7).
467	
468	    The beat dispatcher (`worker.tasks.dispatch_due_schedules`) ticks every
469	    minute and queries `enabled AND next_run_at <= now()` — an indexed scan, so
470	    the cron is parsed only when a schedule actually fires, never per tick.
471	
472	    `next_run_at` is precomputed (`services.cron.next_fire`) on create and after
473	    each fire / cron change. **No-backfill semantics**: a fire advances it to the
474	    next *future* occurrence, so a downtime gap fires at most once on recovery
475	    rather than backfilling every missed slot (correct for monitoring). `cron` is
476	    a standard 5-field expression evaluated in `timezone` (IANA, DST-aware,
477	    default UTC). Cascade-deleted with the suite.
478	    """
479	
480	    __tablename__ = "schedules"
481	    __table_args__ = (
482	        Index("ix_schedules_suite_id", "suite_id"),
483	        # The dispatcher's hot path: due + enabled schedules, oldest-due first.
484	        Index("ix_schedules_enabled_next_run_at", "enabled", "next_run_at"),
485	    )
486	
487	    id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = _uuid_pk()
488	    suite_id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = mapped_column(
489	        UUID(as_uuid=True), ForeignKey("suites.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), nullable=False
490	    )
491	    cron: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(128), nullable=False)
492	    # IANA tz name the cron is evaluated in (e.g. 'America/New_York'); 'UTC' default.
493	    timezone: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64), nullable=False, server_default=text("'UTC'"))
494	    enabled: Mapped[bool] = mapped_column(Boolean, nullable=False, server_default=text("true"))
495	    # Precomputed next fire (UTC). The dispatcher scans on this; never parses cron.
496	    next_run_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=False)
497	    # Last time the dispatcher fired this schedule (NULL until first fire).
498	    last_run_at: Mapped[datetime | None] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True))
499	    created_by: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = mapped_column(
500	        UUID(as_uuid=True), ForeignKey("users.id"), nullable=False
501	    )
502	    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = _created_at()
503	    updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = _updated_at()
504	
505	
506	class SuiteNotification(Base):
507	    """Per-suite alert delivery config (one row per suite).
508	
509	    Decides *whether* a suite's run outcomes are delivered (``enabled``), at what
510	    threshold (``alert_on``), and *where* — an optional per-suite Teams webhook
511	    referenced via ``webhook_secret_ref`` (the URL is token-bearing, so it lives
512	    in the SecretStore, never the DB), falling back to the workspace webhook when
513	    NULL. Suites with no row use the default policy (alert on warn+). The Teams
514	    publisher reads this when delivering (``alerting.teams``). Cascade-deleted
515	    with the suite.
516	    """
517	
518	    __tablename__ = "suite_notifications"
519	    __table_args__ = (
520	        _in_check("alert_on", ALERT_ON_POLICIES, "alert_on_valid"),
521	        UniqueConstraint("suite_id", name="uq_suite_notifications_suite_id"),
522	    )
523	
524	    id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = _uuid_pk()
525	    suite_id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = mapped_column(
526	        UUID(as_uuid=True), ForeignKey("suites.id", ondelete="CASCADE"), nullable=False
527	    )
528	    enabled: Mapped[bool] = mapped_column(Boolean, nullable=False, server_default=text("true"))
529	    # Delivery threshold (ADR 0005 tiers): 'fail' = fail/critical only, 'warn' =
530	    # warn+, 'always' = every terminal run. Default 'warn' matches the no-config
531	    # fallback (`notification_service.DEFAULT_ALERT_ON`) so saving a config
532	    # doesn't silently change the threshold. See `alerting.routing.route_for`.
533	    alert_on: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(16), nullable=False, server_default=text("'warn'"))
534	    # SecretStore key for the per-suite Teams webhook URL (NULL → workspace webhook).
535	    # The URL is a secret; only the ref is stored here.
536	    webhook_secret_ref: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(256))
537	    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = _created_at()
538	    updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = _updated_at()
539	
540	
541	__all__ = [
542	    "Base",
543	    "Check",
544	    "Connection",
545	    "PipelineRun",
546	    "Result",
547	    "Run",
548	    "Schedule",
549	    "Share",
550	    "Suite",
551	    "SuiteNotification",
552	    "TriggerBinding",
553	    "User",
554	]
555

(elided from the diagram for noise):** every table has a `gen_random_uuid()` UUID PK and `created_at`; mutable entities also carry `updated_at`. Status/type columns are `TEXT` + `CHECK` constraints, **not** native PG enums (migration ergonomics).\n- **Cascade posture (ADR [0020](adr/0020-history-and-audit-strategy.md)):** deleting a suite cascades its checks, runs, results, shares, trigger bindings, schedules, and notification config; deleting a connection cascades its version history. History is not retained past entity deletion — accepted. Version snapshots survive their *author* (`changed_by` is `SET NULL`), not their entity.\n- **`pipeline_runs` ≠ `runs` — no FK between them.** Orchestrator pipeline executions correlate to the DQ suite runs they trigger only via the string marker `runs.triggered_by = '<provider>:<pipeline_or_dag_id>:<provider_run_id>'` (dotted lines above); `trigger_bindings` matches pipeline runs by `(provider, pipeline_or_dag_id, env)`, also without an FK. A partial unique index on `runs (suite_id, triggered_by)` dedupes orchestration-triggered runs.\n- **Singleton constraints:** at most one orchestrator connection per `(type, env)` (partial unique index over `adf`/`airflow` only — datasources may repeat); one `suite_notifications` row per suite; one live `shares` row per `(suite, user)`.\n- **Secrets are never in these tables.** `connections.secret_ref` / `suite_notifications.webhook_secret_ref` hold SecretStore *keys*; version snapshots deliberately omit credentials, so a credential rotation records no version.\n\n## Key invariants"
}

> AGENT

Added a **Data model (ER diagram)** section to [architecture.md](docs/architecture.md), generated from the actual SQLAlchemy models in [models.py](backend/app/db/models.py) and validated with the Mermaid renderer (it parses clean as an `erDiagram`, so it will render on GitHub).

What's in it:

- **All 13 tables** with their meaningful columns, PK/FK/UK markers, and inline comments for the constrained value sets (connection types, run/result statuses, check kinds, permissions, alert policies). Boilerplate `id`/`created_at`/`updated_at` conventions are stated once in prose instead of repeated per table, to keep the diagram readable.
- **Real FK relationships** with their delete behavior labeled — the suite-rooted CASCADE tree (checks, runs, results, shares, trigger_bindings, schedules, suite_notifications), connection→version history, and the `SET NULL` author links on the two version-snapshot tables.
- **The two soft (no-FK) correlations drawn as dotted lines** — `pipeline_runs ⇢ runs` via the `triggered_by` string marker and `trigger_bindings ⇢ pipeline_runs` via the `(provider, pipeline_or_dag_id, env)` match — since the `pipeline_runs ≠ runs` distinction is the thing this codebase most wants readers not to get wrong.
- A short **Reading notes** subsection covering the cascade posture (ADR 0020), the orchestrator-singleton partial unique index, and the "secrets are refs, never values" rule.

I also updated the doc's header note to say "diagrams" (plural) so the sync-in-same-PR rule explicitly covers the ER diagram, and the new section carries its own pointer to `models.py` as the source of truth.

The change is uncommitted on `main` — say the word if you want me to branch, commit (`docs: add data-model ER diagram to architecture.md`), and open a PR per the usual flow.

Exit code 1
# Observability & troubleshooting

## Logs & telemetry

- **Structured JSON logs** (structlog) everywhere, with a `request_id` correlated across
  FastAPI → Celery → Great Expectations. **PII is redacted at the logger level**, so
  failed-check sample rows can't leak into logs.
- **Azure Application Insights** captures logs + exceptions in deployed environments
  (gated on the connection string; off locally).
- **Request + task spans** (OpenTelemetry, Azure-exported) in deployed environments:
  every API request and Celery task run is a trace in App Insights, linked
  request → task, with a `dataq.request_id` attribute joining spans to the log lines.
  Health probes and the secret-bearing webhook URLs are excluded by design.

## Where to look

- **Dashboard** — workspace health score, pass-rate, run counts, and trends.
- **Results** — every run, drill into per-check pass/fail with observed-vs-expected and
  (PII-redacted) sample failing rows; a **Pipelines** tab correlates orchestration runs.
- **Run progress** — live check-by-check status for an in-flight run.

## Common failures

| Symptom | Likely cause / fix |
|---|---|
| Run stuck `queued` | Broker (Redis) unreachable at dispatch. A beat **reaper** fails runs left non-terminal past a threshold; re-run once the worker/broker is healthy. |
| Connection **Test** fails | Bad/expired credential or network egress — re-authenticate the connection; check the secret store. |
| Pipeline didn't trigger a suite | Only **successful** runs trigger; confirm an **enabled trigger binding** matches `(provider, pipeline/DAG, env)` exactly (the DAG/pipeline id is case-sensitive). |
| Checks all `skip` on a flat-file suite | The batch hasn't landed yet (no matching file) — expected; the run succeeds with skips. |
| Orchestration events not arriving | Webhook secret/HMAC mismatch — the 10-min poll is the fallback; verify the connection's secret, and copy the URL from **Settings → Webhooks** instead of assembling it. |

> AGENT

Good question — I inventoried `docs/`, `deploy/`, and `.github/` first. The repo is already well past standard practice on most fronts (ADRs with an index, runbook/FAQ, observability guide, deployment guide, compliance posture, glossary, feature matrix, retro, SECURITY.md, Swagger for the API surface). The real gaps are narrow, and they're all in the same family as the ER diagram: **the docs describe structure well but never show behavior over time.** Ranked by value:

1. **Sequence diagrams for the two load-bearing runtime flows.** Nothing in the repo shows ordering/time — the flowchart shows who talks to whom, the ER diagram shows what's stored, but the choreography lives only in prose scattered across ADRs. The two that earn their keep:
   - **Suite run lifecycle:** trigger (manual / schedule / webhook) → API → Celery dispatch → GX execution → severity derivation (ADR 0016) → results → `ResultPublisher` alert routing. This crosses every component and is the flow a new contributor has to reconstruct by reading five services.
   - **Orchestration event flow:** ADF/Airflow webhook → auth check (secret / HMAC) → `pipeline_runs` upsert → trigger-binding match → run dispatch, with the 10-min poll fallback and the dedup index as annotations. It's the flow with the most "critical pointers" warnings in CLAUDE.md, which is a signal it's under-diagrammed.

2. **A state diagram for run and result statuses.** Tiny, but the semantics are subtle and load-bearing: `skip`/`error` are orthogonal to the four severity tiers and must be excluded from the health-score N — that rule currently lives in a code comment in [models.py:27-35](backend/app/db/models.py#L27-L35). A ~15-line `stateDiagram` in architecture.md (or concepts.md) makes it un-missable.

3. **A drift guard for the ER diagram.** Best practice for hand-drawn ERDs is either generate-from-metadata or test-against-metadata. The "update in the same PR" note relies on discipline; a small pytest that asserts every `Base.metadata` table name appears in `architecture.md` (and flags tables in the doc that no longer exist) would catch drift in CI for ~20 lines. Column-level checking is overkill; table-level is the right altitude.

4. **A CHANGELOG / release notes.** `v1.0.0` is tagged but there's no `CHANGELOG.md` and (as far as the repo shows) no GitHub Release body. With conventional commits enforced, generating one is nearly free, and it matters for the BYOL/marketplace ambition (ADR 0013) — customers deploying your image will ask "what changed in v1.1?"

5. **A trust-boundary / auth-flow diagram** (threat-model lite). The auth story is complete but scattered across ADRs 0006, 0007, 0008, and 0028: browser OIDC, MCP bearer, webhook secret-in-URL vs HMAC, internal-vs-public ingress. One diagram showing the trust boundaries would consolidate it — and it's the artifact a marketplace security review or customer security questionnaire asks for first.

Things I'd explicitly *not* add: full C4 formality (your flowchart already is a container diagram; more layers is ceremony), a hand-written API reference (Swagger owns that), and component-level class diagrams of the service layer (they rot fast and the code is readable).

If you want, I can do #1 and #2 now — they're pure additions to architecture.md in the same style as the ER diagram, validated the same way. #3 is a small test PR, #4 is a one-off backfill plus a release-notes habit, and #5 is probably worth doing as part of the pre-marketplace hardening rather than today.

> DEVELOPER

do all five. The point four i'm thinking more of for major [maybe including some minor] releases not for every PR, what say you?

> AGENT

All five it is. On the point-4 cadence question, my answer up front: **agree — release-time, not per-PR.** Per-PR changelog lines are exactly the kind of churn Keep-a-Changelog warns about (merge conflicts, entries nobody curates), and your squash-merge + conventional-commits discipline means `git log v1.0.0..v1.1.0 --oneline` regenerates the raw material on demand. So the policy I'll encode: **one curated entry per minor/major release at tag time; patch releases only get an entry when user-visible or security-relevant.** I'll write that into the CHANGELOG header and the release checklist in the runbook.

Now let me ground the sequence/state/trust diagrams in the actual code so they're accurate, not aspirational.

1	"""Celery tasks for asynchronous suite execution.
2	
3	``run_suite`` is the worker entry point dispatched by the manual-run / probe /
4	pipeline-trigger paths. It loads the run's suite / connection / checks, resolves
5	the suite's datasource-shaped **target** (#215) to the runner's
6	``(table, schema, catalog)``, builds the datasource adapter, and hands off to
7	``run_service.execute_run``. The DB-touching core is factored into ``_run_suite``
8	so it can be unit-tested with a fake session + fake runner (no Postgres, no
9	Snowflake); real-DB integration coverage is a Week 8 item.
10	
11	The target lives on the suite (``Suite.target``, resolved by
12	``run_target.resolve_target``): a targetless suite drives the run to ``failed``
13	with a clear log rather than running against an unknown table.
14	"""
15	
16	from __future__ import annotations
17	
18	import uuid
19	from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
20	
21	from sqlalchemy import select
22	from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
23	
24	from backend.app.alerting import dispatch as alert_dispatch
25	from backend.app.core.config import get_settings
26	from backend.app.core.errors import DataQError
27	from backend.app.core.logging import get_logger
28	from backend.app.core.secrets import SecretStore, get_secret_store
29	from backend.app.datasources.flatfile import BatchNotFoundError
30	from backend.app.datasources.registry import build_check_runner
31	from backend.app.db.models import (
32	    ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS,
33	    Check,
34	    Connection,
35	    Run,
36	    Schedule,
37	    Suite,
38	)
39	from backend.app.db.session import get_session
40	from backend.app.orchestration.registry import get_orchestration_provider
41	from backend.app.services import cron, orchestration_service, run_dispatch, run_service, run_target
42	from backend.app.worker.celery_app import celery_app
43	
44	# Polling fallback (#171): look back slightly further than the 10-min beat
45	# interval so a run can't slip through the gap between consecutive polls.
46	_POLL_LOOKBACK = timedelta(minutes=15)
47	# Gap recovery (B2): a wider window swept on startup + every 30 min to re-ingest
48	# runs missed while the system was down (worker/beat restart, webhook + poll both
49	# offline). Same provider-agnostic pipeline; only the lookback differs. Safe to
50	# overlap the regular poll — the upsert is idempotent and `skip_updated_since`
51	# drops runs already recorded inside the window.
52	_GAP_RECOVERY_LOOKBACK = timedelta(hours=1)
53	
54	log = get_logger(__name__)
55	
56	
57	def _terminal_failed(session: Session, run: Run, *, event: str, run_id: uuid.UUID) -> str:
58	    """Drive ``run`` to terminal ``failed`` (never left ``queued``/``running``)."""
59	    run.status = "failed"
60	    run.started_at = run.started_at or datetime.now(UTC)
61	    run.finished_at = datetime.now(UTC)
62	    session.commit()
63	    log.exception(event, run_id=str(run_id))
64	    return "failed"
65	
66	
67	def _run_suite(session: Session, *, run_id: uuid.UUID) -> str:
68	    """Load the run's graph, resolve its target, build the runner, execute.
69	
70	    The suite's datasource-shaped ``target`` (#215) resolves to the runner's
71	    ``(table, schema, catalog)`` via ``run_target.resolve_target``; dispatch by
72	    ``connection.type`` through the runner registry gives a Snowflake / Unity
73	    Catalog / flat-file suite its correct `CheckRunner` (#146). A flat-file *batch*
74	    target is then materialized to a concrete path by listing the store
75	    (`materialize_path`).
76	
77	    Failures while loading, resolving the target (targetless or malformed suite),
78	    or building the runner (missing rows, bad connection config, unresolved
79	    secret) drive the run to ``failed`` so it never lingers in ``queued``;
80	    execution failures are handled inside ``execute_run``. A genuinely-absent
81	    batch (`BatchNotFoundError`) is **not** a failure — the data hasn't landed, so
82	    every check is ``skip``ped (#122) and the run succeeds.
83	    """
84	    run = session.get(Run, run_id)
85	    if run is None:
86	        log.error("run_suite_run_not_found", run_id=str(run_id))
87	        return "not_found"
88	
89	    # Cooperative cancellation: a cancel that landed while the run was queued (or
90	    # in the dispatch→pickup window) already set 'cancelled' — don't execute it.
91	    # (revoke also drops a still-queued task; this is the belt-and-braces check.)
92	    if run.status == "cancelled":
93	        log.info("run_suite_already_cancelled", run_id=str(run_id))
94	        return "cancelled"
95	
96	    try:
97	        suite = session.get(Suite, run.suite_id)
98	        connection = session.get(Connection, suite.connection_id) if suite is not None else None
99	        if suite is None or connection is None:
100	            raise RuntimeError("suite or connection not found for run")
101	        target = run_target.resolve_target(connection.type, suite.target)
102	        checks = list(session.scalars(select(Check).where(Check.suite_id == suite.id)))
103	        runner = build_check_runner(
104	            conn_type=connection.type,
105	            config=connection.config,
106	            secret_ref=connection.secret_ref,
107	            secret_store=get_secret_store(),
108	            catalog=target.catalog,
109	        )
110	    except Exception:
111	        return _terminal_failed(session, run, event="run_suite_setup_failed", run_id=run_id)
112	
113	    # Materialize the concrete path (live for a flat-file batch target). Kept
114	    # separate from setup so a missing batch is a skip, not a setup failure.
115	    try:
116	        table = run_target.materialize_path(
117	            connection.type,
118	            connection.config,
119	            target,
120	            secret_ref=connection.secret_ref,
121	            secret_store=get_secret_store(),
122	        )
123	    except BatchNotFoundError:
124	        run_service.skip_run(session, run=run, checks=checks, reason="batch_not_found")
125	        log.info("run_suite_skipped_no_batch", run_id=str(run_id), suite_id=str(suite.id))
126	        return str(run.status)
127	    except Exception:
128	        return _terminal_failed(session, run, event="run_suite_materialize_failed", run_id=run_id)
129	
130	    # The suite's identifier column (#415) — requested from GX so failing rows are
131	    # captured with a locator. A `None`/absent policy keeps the scalar-only sample.
132	    policy = suite.column_policy or {}
133	    identifier = policy.get("identifier_column")
134	    index_columns = [str(identifier)] if identifier else None
135	
136	    run_service.execute_run(
137	        session,
138	        run=run,
139	        checks=checks,
140	        runner=runner,
141	        table=table,
142	        schema=target.schema,
143	        index_columns=index_columns,
144	    )
145	    return str(run.status)
146	
147	
148	@celery_app.task(name="run_suite")  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery task decorator is unannotated
149	def run_suite(run_id: str) -> str:
150	    """Worker entry point. ``run_id`` is a string so it serialises over JSON.
151	
152	    The target is resolved from the suite (``Suite.target``), so the only
153	    argument the dispatcher supplies is the run id.
154	
155	    After the run reaches a terminal state, its outcome is published through the
156	    ``ResultPublisher`` seam (ADR 0011). The publish is best-effort and isolated
157	    (``publish_run_outcome`` never raises), so a notification failure can't
158	    affect the already-persisted run or the task's return value.
159	    """
160	    rid = uuid.UUID(run_id)
161	    session = get_session()
162	    try:
163	        outcome = _run_suite(session, run_id=rid)
164	        alert_dispatch.publish_run_outcome(session, run_id=rid)
165	        return outcome
166	    finally:
167	        session.close()
168	
169	
170	def _poll_orchestration_runs(
171	    session: Session,
172	    *,
173	    secret_store: SecretStore,
174	    now: datetime | None = None,
175	    lookback: timedelta = _POLL_LOOKBACK,
176	    provider: str | None = None,
177	    resource_name: str | None = None,
178	) -> dict[str, int]:
179	    """Poll every orchestrator connection for recent succeeded runs (#171, ADR 0004).
180	
181	    The polling fallback for runs that never produced a webhook: iterate each
182	    ADF / Airflow connection, ask the provider's `list_recent_runs` for runs
183	    updated within the ``lookback`` window, and hand them to `ingest_polled_runs`
184	    (upsert + trigger-on-success). Goes through the `OrchestrationProvider` seam —
185	    no per-provider branching. Each connection is isolated: a transport/auth
186	    failure logs + continues so one bad connection can't starve the rest.
187	
188	    ``lookback`` widens for gap recovery (B2): the same sweep over a 1-hour window
189	    re-ingests runs missed during downtime. ``skip_updated_since`` rides the same
190	    window, so a run we already recorded inside it is skipped while a genuinely
191	    missed one (no row) is upserted.
192	
193	    ``provider`` / ``resource_name`` narrow the sweep for alert-triggered
194	    poll-now calls (#492): an `AlertPing` names the provider (and usually the
195	    factory), so only the matching connection(s) are polled — an alert storm
196	    can't amplify into repeated full sweeps of every orchestrator. The match
197	    rides the provider's ``resource_config_key`` seam, no provider branching.
198	    """
199	    since = (now or datetime.now(UTC)) - lookback
200	    summary = {"connections": 0, "recorded": 0, "triggered": 0, "skipped": 0, "errors": 0}
201	    provider_filter = (
202	        [provider] if provider in ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS else list(ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS)
203	    )
204	    connections = list(
205	        session.scalars(
206	            select(Connection).where(
207	                Connection.type.in_(provider_filter),
208	                Connection.secret_ref.isnot(None),
209	            )
210	        )
211	    )
212	    for connection in connections:
213	        if not connection.secret_ref:  # defensive; the query already filters
214	            continue
215	        try:
216	            provider_impl = get_orchestration_provider(connection.type)
217	            if resource_name is not None and (
218	                connection.config.get(provider_impl.resource_config_key) != resource_name
219	            ):
220	                continue
221	            secret = secret_store.get(connection.secret_ref)
222	            updates = provider_impl.list_recent_runs(dict(connection.config), secret, since)
223	            result = orchestration_service.ingest_polled_runs(
224	                session,
225	                provider_impl=provider_impl,
226	                connection=connection,
227	                updates=updates,
228	                skip_updated_since=since,
229	            )
230	            summary["connections"] += 1
231	            summary["recorded"] += len(result.pipeline_runs)
232	            summary["triggered"] += len(result.triggered_runs)
233	            summary["skipped"] += result.skipped
234	        except Exception:
235	            summary["errors"] += 1
236	            session.rollback()
237	            log.exception(
238	                "orchestration_poll_failed",
239	                connection_id=str(connection.id),
240	                provider=connection.type,
241	            )
242	    log.info("orchestration_poll_completed", **summary)
243	    return summary
244	
245	
246	def _run_orchestration_poll(
247	    lookback: timedelta,
248	    *,
249	    provider: str | None = None,
250	    resource_name: str | None = None,
251	) -> dict[str, int]:
252	    """Open a session, run the poll core over ``lookback``, always close.
253	
254	    Shared by the beat entry points (regular poll + gap recovery) and the
255	    alert-triggered poll-now path (#492) so the session lifecycle lives in one
256	    place — what varies is the window and the optional targeting.
257	    """
258	    session = get_session()
259	    try:
260	        return _poll_orchestration_runs(
261	            session,
262	            secret_store=get_secret_store(),
263	            lookback=lookback,
264	            provider=provider,
265	            resource_name=resource_name,
266	        )
267	    finally:
268	        session.close()
269	
270	
271	@celery_app.task(name="poll_orchestration_runs")  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery task decorator is unannotated
272	def poll_orchestration_runs(
273	    provider: str | None = None, resource_name: str | None = None
274	) -> dict[str, int]:
275	    """The 10-min beat polling fallback; also the alert-triggered poll-now
276	    (#492), where ``provider``/``resource_name`` narrow the sweep to the
277	    alerting orchestrator."""
278	    return _run_orchestration_poll(_POLL_LOOKBACK, provider=provider, resource_name=resource_name)
279	
280	
281	@celery_app.task(name="recover_orchestration_gaps")  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery task decorator is unannotated
282	def recover_orchestration_gaps() -> dict[str, int]:
283	    """Celery-beat entry point — gap recovery (B2), startup + every 30 min.
284	
285	    The same poll pipeline over the wider ``_GAP_RECOVERY_LOOKBACK`` window, to
286	    re-ingest runs missed while the system was down. Idempotent with the regular
287	    poll (upsert + `skip_updated_since`).
288	    """
289	    return _run_orchestration_poll(_GAP_RECOVERY_LOOKBACK)
290	
291	
292	# ──────────────────────── scheduled run dispatch (A7) ──────────────────────
293	
294	
295	def _advance_schedule(schedule: Schedule, *, now: datetime) -> bool:
296	    """Roll ``schedule`` forward to its next future fire and stamp ``last_run_at``.
297	
298	    **No-backfill semantics**: ``cron.next_fire`` returns the next occurrence
299	    strictly after ``now``, so a gap (worker/beat down across several slots) is
300	    collapsed to a single fire rather than backfilled. Returns True if advanced;
301	    False (and disables the schedule) if the stored cron/tz is somehow invalid —
302	    validated on write, so this only guards against direct DB tampering and stops
303	    an un-advanceable row from hot-looping the dispatcher every tick.
304	    """
305	    schedule.last_run_at = now
306	    try:
307	        schedule.next_run_at = cron.next_fire(schedule.cron, schedule.timezone, after=now)
308	    except DataQError:
309	        schedule.enabled = False
310	        log.error(
311	            "schedule_disabled_invalid_cron",
312	            schedule_id=str(schedule.id),
313	            cron=schedule.cron,
314	            timezone=schedule.timezone,
315	        )
316	        return False
317	    return True
318	
319	
320	def _fire_schedule(session: Session, schedule: Schedule, *, now: datetime) -> str:
321	    """Fire one due schedule: advance it, then queue + dispatch a suite run.
322	
323	    Advancing ``next_run_at`` happens **before** the run is created and is
324	    committed in every branch, so the schedule leaves the due window for this
325	    tick whatever the run's fate — a misconfigured suite never hot-loops. The run
326	    is created with the canonical ``schedule:<id>`` ``triggered_by`` marker and
327	    handed to the worker exactly like the manual / pipeline-trigger paths; a
328	    targetless suite is skipped (not queued-then-failed), and a broker outage
329	    marks the run ``failed`` rather than leaving it stuck ``queued`` (#227).
330	    """
331	    if not _advance_schedule(schedule, now=now):
332	        session.commit()
333	        return "disabled"
334	
335	    suite = session.get(Suite, schedule.suite_id)
336	    assert suite is not None  # schedule cascade-deletes with its suite
337	    connection = session.get(Connection, suite.connection_id)
338	    assert connection is not None  # suite.connection_id FK is RESTRICT
339	    try:
340	        run_target.resolve_target(connection.type, suite.target)
341	    except DataQError:
342	        session.commit()  # persist the advance; skip the doomed run
343	        log.warning(
344	            "schedule_skipped_invalid_target",
345	            schedule_id=str(schedule.id),
346	            suite_id=str(suite.id),
347	        )
348	        return "skipped_target"
349	
350	    run = Run(suite_id=suite.id, status="queued", triggered_by=f"schedule:{schedule.id}")
351	    session.add(run)
352	    session.commit()
353	    session.refresh(run)
354	    # Shared dispatch+broker-failure block (#227): on failure the run is marked
355	    # terminal-`failed` and logged (with schedule_id kept on the event); the
356	    # advance is already committed, so the schedule has left the due window.
357	    if not run_dispatch.dispatch_or_fail(session, run, schedule_id=str(schedule.id)):
358	        return "dispatch_failed"
359	    log.info("schedule_fired", schedule_id=str(schedule.id), run_id=str(run.id))
360	    return "dispatched"
361	
362	
363	def _dispatch_due_schedules(session: Session, *, now: datetime | None = None) -> dict[str, int]:
364	    """Fire every enabled schedule whose ``next_run_at`` has passed (A7).
365	
366	    Pulls due schedules one at a time with ``FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`` so two
367	    overlapping dispatcher ticks can't double-fire the same schedule: the second
368	    skips a row the first holds, and once fired the row's ``next_run_at`` is past
369	    ``now`` so it drops out of the due set. ``now`` is fixed at entry, so the loop
370	    is finite (each iteration advances one row out of the window).
371	    """
372	    now = now or datetime.now(UTC)
373	    summary = {"due": 0, "dispatched": 0, "skipped_target": 0, "dispatch_failed": 0, "disabled": 0}
374	    while True:
375	        schedule = session.scalars(
376	            select(Schedule)
377	            .where(Schedule.enabled.is_(True), Schedule.next_run_at <= now)
378	            .order_by(Schedule.next_run_at)
379	            .with_for_update(skip_locked=True)
380	            .limit(1)
381	        ).first()
382	        if schedule is None:
383	            break
384	        summary["due"] += 1
385	        outcome = _fire_schedule(session, schedule, now=now)
386	        summary[outcome] = summary.get(outcome, 0) + 1
387	    log.info("schedules_dispatch_completed", **summary)
388	    return summary
389	
390	
391	@celery_app.task(name="dispatch_due_schedules")  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery task decorator is unannotated
392	def dispatch_due_schedules() -> dict[str, int]:
393	    """Celery-beat entry point — fire due suite-run schedules (A7), every minute."""
394	    session = get_session()
395	    try:
396	        return _dispatch_due_schedules(session)
397	    finally:
398	        session.close()
399	
400	
401	# ─────────────────────── result retention sweep (PII purge) ─────────────────
402	
403	
404	@celery_app.task(name="purge_sample_failures")  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery task decorator is unannotated
405	def purge_sample_failures() -> int:
406	    """Celery-beat entry point — daily PII-retention sweep.
407	
408	    Scrubs `sample_failures` from results older than the configured
409	    ``sample_failures_retention_days`` window (keeping the row + `metric_value` so
410	    trends survive — ADR 0012). Returns the number of rows scrubbed.
411	    """
412	    session = get_session()
413	    try:
414	        retention_days = get_settings().sample_failures_retention_days
415	        return run_service.purge_expired_sample_failures(session, retention_days=retention_days)
416	    finally:
417	        session.close()
418	
419	
420	# ──────────────────────── stuck-run reaper (#309) ──────────────────────────
421	
422	
423	@celery_app.task(name="reap_stuck_runs")  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery task decorator is unannotated
424	def reap_stuck_runs() -> int:
425	    """Celery-beat entry point — fail runs orphaned in a non-terminal state (#309).
426	
427	    A run committed ``queued`` before its task was published — or left ``running``
428	    by a worker that died mid-execution — would otherwise linger forever (gap
429	    recovery only covers ``pipeline_runs``). The reaper drives such runs, stuck
430	    past ``stuck_run_threshold_minutes``, to terminal ``failed`` so they surface in
431	    the runs table / dashboard and the user can re-run. No alert is published — see
432	    ``run_service.reap_stuck_runs`` for why (a reaped run is an infra/liveness
433	    event, and alerting a slow-but-alive run would be an irreversible false alarm).
434	    Returns the count reaped.
435	    """
436	    session = get_session()
437	    try:
438	        threshold = get_settings().stuck_run_threshold_minutes
439	        return len(run_service.reap_stuck_runs(session, threshold_minutes=threshold))
440	    finally:
441	        session.close()
442

1	"""Execute a suite's checks via a datasource adapter and persist the results.
2	
3	This is the GX-agnostic core of a run: it drives the `Run` lifecycle, calls a
4	`CheckRunner` (injected — Snowflake in production, a fake in tests), and maps the
5	returned `SuiteOutcome` onto `Result` rows. GX/Snowflake specifics live behind
6	the adapter; this layer only knows the DTOs in ``datasources.base``.
7	
8	Run.status describes *execution*, not data quality: a run that completes is
9	``succeeded`` even when checks fail (the failures live in ``Result.status`` /
10	``SuiteOutcome.success``). ``failed`` means the run could not execute — the
11	adapter raised (e.g. could not reach the warehouse).
12	"""
13	
14	from __future__ import annotations
15	
16	import uuid
17	from collections import defaultdict
18	from collections.abc import Mapping, Sequence
19	from dataclasses import dataclass
20	from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
21	from typing import Any, cast
22	
23	from sqlalchemy import CursorResult, func, null, select, update
24	from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
25	
26	from backend.app.core.jsonsafe import sanitize_json
27	from backend.app.core.logging import get_logger
28	from backend.app.datasources.base import (
29	    CheckOutcome,
30	    CheckRunner,
31	    CheckSpec,
32	    MonitorRunner,
33	    MonitorSpec,
34	)
35	from backend.app.datasources.monitors import MONITOR_KINDS
36	from backend.app.db.models import RESULT_STATUSES, RUN_STATUSES, Check, Result, Run
37	from backend.app.services import run_dispatch, suite_service
38	from backend.app.services.column_classification import ColumnClass, classify_column, is_sensitive
39	from backend.app.services.severity import resolve_status
40	
41	log = get_logger(__name__)
42	
43	
44	def _now() -> datetime:
45	    return datetime.now(UTC)
46	
47	
48	def _build_result(run_id: uuid.UUID, check: Check, outcome: CheckOutcome) -> Result:
49	    """Map a check + its GX outcome to a `Result`, deriving the severity tier.
50	
51	    The unexpected-percent badness scalar is extracted once and used both to band
52	    the tier (ADR 0005 / 0016) and to persist as the durable, SQL-aggregatable
53	    `metric_value` (ADR 0012). `duration_ms` stays NULL in v1 — per-check timing
54	    isn't separable from GX's single suite-level `validate()` (reserved seam).
55	
56	    A check the runner could not *evaluate* (`outcome.errored` — e.g. it raised
57	    referencing a missing column) is an operational ``error`` result (#122), not a
58	    data failure: no severity tier, no `metric_value`. It's orthogonal to the
59	    health score (ADR 0005 weights only the four tiers), so it must never be
60	    banded as `fail`. The error message lands in `observed_value` for debugging —
61	    GX exception messages are schema-level (no row data), so they don't go through
62	    the `sample_failures` retention/PII path.
63	    """
64	    status, metric = resolve_status(
65	        outcome,
66	        warn_threshold=check.warn_threshold,
67	        fail_threshold=check.fail_threshold,
68	        critical_threshold=check.critical_threshold,
69	    )
70	    if outcome.errored:
71	        # An errored check has no observed metric and no failing-row sample; surface
72	        # the (schema-level, row-data-free) GX message for debugging instead.
73	        observed = {"error": outcome.error_message} if outcome.error_message else None
74	        sample = None
75	    else:
76	        observed = sanitize_json(outcome.observed_value)
77	        sample = sanitize_json(outcome.sample_failures)
78	    return Result(
79	        run_id=run_id,
80	        check_id=check.id,
81	        status=status,
82	        metric_value=metric,
83	        observed_value=observed,
84	        expected_value=sanitize_json(outcome.expected_value),
85	        sample_failures=sample,
86	    )
87	
88	
89	_EXPECTATION_KIND = "expectation"
90	
91	
92	def _run_outcomes(
93	    runner: CheckRunner,
94	    *,
95	    table: str,
96	    schema: str | None,
97	    checks: list[Check],
98	    index_columns: list[str] | None = None,
99	) -> list[CheckOutcome]:
100	    """Run a suite's checks, dispatching by `check.kind` (ADR 0012), and return one
101	    outcome per check in the **same order** (so they zip 1:1 onto result rows).
102	
103	    * ``expectation`` kind → the GX `CheckRunner.run_checks`.
104	    * ``freshness``/``volume`` (monitor kinds) → the `MonitorRunner.run_monitors`
105	      SQL path — only when the runner is a SQL datasource (Snowflake/UC). A monitor
106	      check on a flat-file runner raises (gated here, not silently mis-run).
107	    * any other reserved kind (`schema_drift`/`anomaly`/`comparison`) has no runner
108	      yet → `NotImplementedError`.
109	
110	    This composes with the connection-type runner selection (ADR 0011): `kind`
111	    chooses the *monitor*, `connection.type` chose the *adapter* (the runner)."""
112	    expectation_idx = [i for i, c in enumerate(checks) if c.kind == _EXPECTATION_KIND]
113	    monitor_idx = [i for i, c in enumerate(checks) if c.kind in MONITOR_KINDS]
114	    unsupported = sorted(
115	        {c.kind for c in checks if c.kind != _EXPECTATION_KIND and c.kind not in MONITOR_KINDS}
116	    )
117	    if unsupported:
118	        raise NotImplementedError(f"no run path for check kind(s) {', '.join(unsupported)}")
119	
120	    outcomes: list[CheckOutcome | None] = [None] * len(checks)
```

1	"""Severity post-processing — derive a result's tier from check thresholds.
2	
3	The single home for the ADR-0005 tier derivation + the ADR-0012 `metric_value`
4	extraction, kept pure (no DB, no GX) so the semantics live in one place: if we
5	ever switch from banding the *unexpected fraction* to banding the *raw observed
6	value* (ADR 0016 approach B), only this module changes.
7	
8	Model (ADR 0016, approach A):
9	- `metric_value` = the GX **unexpected percent** (0-100): how badly the rule was
10	  violated. 0 = clean, higher = worse. It is the SQL-aggregatable badness scalar
11	  (ADR 0012) and the quantity the thresholds band.
12	- When a check has thresholds, they are the user's severity policy and they
13	  fully determine the tier — overriding GX's binary success (e.g. a check with
14	  `mostly=0.99` that GX fails at 0.5% unexpected resolves to `pass` if the user's
15	  `warn` threshold is 1%). The stored `metric_value` keeps this transparent.
16	- No thresholds set, or no metric to band (aggregate checks like row_count that
17	  produce no unexpected fraction) → binary `pass`/`fail` from GX success
18	  (ADR 0005 binary fallback).
19	"""
20	
21	from __future__ import annotations
22	
23	from decimal import Decimal, InvalidOperation
24	from typing import Any
25	
26	from backend.app.datasources.base import CheckOutcome
27	
28	# GX key carrying the violated-row fraction (0-100), copied into sample_failures
29	# by the runner. This is the badness scalar the thresholds band.
30	_UNEXPECTED_PERCENT_KEY = "unexpected_percent"
31	
32	
33	def extract_metric(outcome: CheckOutcome) -> Decimal | None:
34	    """The numeric badness scalar for a check, or None if it has none.
35	
36	    A *monitor* (freshness/volume, ADR 0012) computes its metric directly, so its
37	    ``outcome.metric_value`` is preferred when set. Otherwise this reads the GX
38	    unexpected-percent from the outcome's sample detail. Computed at run time and
39	    persisted to `results.metric_value`, so it survives the later sample-failures
40	    retention purge (the durable scalar the dashboard trends). Uses
41	    ``Decimal(str(...))`` so a float like ``0.5`` lands as exact ``0.5`` in the
42	    NUMERIC column rather than its binary expansion.
43	    """
44	    raw: Any
45	    if outcome.metric_value is not None:
46	        raw = outcome.metric_value
47	    else:
48	        sample: dict[str, Any] | None = outcome.sample_failures
49	        if not sample or _UNEXPECTED_PERCENT_KEY not in sample:
50	            return None
51	        raw = sample[_UNEXPECTED_PERCENT_KEY]
52	        if raw is None:
53	            return None
54	    try:
55	        metric = Decimal(str(raw))
56	    except (InvalidOperation, ValueError, TypeError):
57	        return None
58	    # GX can emit NaN/inf — e.g. unexpected_percent on an empty table is 0/0
59	    # (the same reason run_service runs a NaN->null sanitizer). A non-finite
60	    # Decimal slips past the parse but would make every threshold comparison
61	    # False (silently 'pass') or raise mid-run; treat it as "no bandable
62	    # metric" so derive_status falls back to binary pass/fail.
63	    return metric if metric.is_finite() else None
64	
65	
66	def resolve_status(
67	    outcome: CheckOutcome,
68	    *,
69	    warn_threshold: Decimal | None,
70	    fail_threshold: Decimal | None,
71	    critical_threshold: Decimal | None,
72	) -> tuple[str, Decimal | None]:
73	    """Resolve a check outcome to its persisted ``(status, metric_value)``.
74	
75	    The single decision both run-result persistence (`run_service`) and the
76	    check-editor dry-run (`dryrun_service`) share, so a preview can never disagree
77	    with the run it previews:
78	
79	    * a check the runner could not *evaluate* (`outcome.errored`, #122) is the
80	      operational ``error`` status — no severity tier, no metric to band; vs.
```

340	def get_run(session: Session, run_id: uuid.UUID) -> Run | None:
341	    """Fetch a run by id (no authz — the API layer gates on the run's suite)."""
342	    return session.get(Run, run_id)
343	
344	
345	_TERMINAL_STATUSES = frozenset({"succeeded", "failed", "cancelled"})
346	# The complement, single-sourced from the canonical status set so a new
347	# lifecycle status can't silently escape the reaper's net (#309).
348	_NON_TERMINAL_STATUSES = frozenset(RUN_STATUSES) - _TERMINAL_STATUSES
349	
350	
351	def cancel_run(session: Session, run: Run) -> bool:
352	    """Transition a non-terminal run to ``cancelled``; return whether it changed.
353	
354	    Returns ``False`` if the run is already terminal (succeeded/failed/cancelled)
355	    — the API surfaces that as 409. Sets ``finished_at``; ``started_at`` is left
356	    as-is (NULL if the run was still queued). This is the DB half; the API layer
357	    also best-effort revokes the Celery task, and the worker honours the
358	    ``cancelled`` status cooperatively (start-check + in-flight guard).
359	    """
360	    if run.status in _TERMINAL_STATUSES:
361	        return False
362	    run.status = "cancelled"
363	    run.finished_at = _now()
364	    session.commit()
365	    log.info("run_cancelled", run_id=str(run.id))
366	    return True
367	
368	
369	def list_results(session: Session, run_id: uuid.UUID) -> list[Result]:
370	    """The result rows for a run, in stable check order (`created_at`)."""
371	    return list(
372	        session.scalars(select(Result).where(Result.run_id == run_id).order_by(Result.created_at))

"""Validates the db_session fixture: real persistence + per-test rollback isolation.

These run only when TEST_DATABASE_URL is set (CI Postgres service / local compose);
they skip otherwise.
"""

from typing import Any

from sqlalchemy import select

from backend.app.db.models import User


def test_db_session_persists_with_server_default_id(db_session: Any) -> None:
    user = User(aad_object_id="fixture-1", email="f1@example.com", display_name="F1")
    db_session.add(user)
    db_session.commit()

    got = db_session.scalars(select(User).where(User.aad_object_id == "fixture-1")).one()
    assert got.email == "f1@example.com"
    assert got.id is not None  # gen_random_uuid() server default executed
    assert got.created_at is not None


def test_isolation_write(db_session: Any) -> None:
    db_session.add(User(aad_object_id="iso", email="iso@example.com", display_name=None))
    db_session.commit()
    assert db_session.scalars(select(User).where(User.aad_object_id == "iso")).one_or_none()

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/docs/architecture.md",
  "old_string": "- **Secrets are never in these tables.** `connections.secret_ref` / `suite_notifications.webhook_secret_ref` hold SecretStore *keys*; version snapshots deliberately omit credentials, so a credential rotation records no version.\n\n## Key invariants",
  "new_string": "- **Secrets are never in these tables.** `connections.secret_ref` / `suite_notifications.webhook_secret_ref` hold SecretStore *keys*; version snapshots deliberately omit credentials, so a credential rotation records no version.\n\n## Runtime flows\n\nThe diagrams above show *structure* (who talks to whom, what is stored); these two show *ordering* for the flows that cross the most components.\n\n### Suite run lifecycle\n\nEvery run — manual (`POST /suites/{id}/run`), scheduled (the 60s beat dispatcher), or orchestration-triggered — converges on the same path once the `Run` row exists:\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    autonumber\n    participant Trig as Trigger<br/>(manual API · schedule beat · pipeline event)\n    participant API as FastAPI / beat dispatcher\n    participant PG as PostgreSQL\n    participant BR as Redis broker\n    participant W as Celery worker (run_suite)\n    participant KV as SecretStore (Key Vault)\n    participant DS as Datasource\n    participant Pub as ResultPublisher (Teams · Slack · email)\n\n    Trig->>API: trigger suite run\n    API->>PG: INSERT runs (status=queued, triggered_by marker)\n    API->>BR: send_task run_suite(run_id)\n    Note over API,BR: broker down → run marked terminal failed,<br/>never left stuck queued (#227)\n    API->>PG: store celery_task_id (enables cancel/revoke)\n\n    BR->>W: deliver task\n    W->>PG: load run — already cancelled? stop (cooperative cancel)\n    W->>PG: load suite + connection + checks, resolve target (#215)\n    W->>KV: get connection credential (secret_ref)\n    W->>W: build CheckRunner by connection.type (registry, ADR 0011)\n    Note over W,KV: any setup failure → run terminal failed\n    W->>DS: materialize flat-file batch path\n    Note over W,DS: batch absent → every check skip,<br/>run still succeeds (#122)\n    W->>PG: UPDATE runs SET status=running\n    W->>DS: execute by check.kind (ADR 0012) —<br/>expectation → GX validate · freshness/volume → monitor SQL\n    DS-->>W: one CheckOutcome per check\n    W->>W: severity.resolve_status — band unexpected-% against<br/>warn/fail/critical thresholds (ADR 0005/0016)\n    W->>PG: INSERT results (status, metric_value, redacted sample_failures)\n    W->>PG: UPDATE runs SET status=succeeded<br/>(failed = the adapter raised)\n    W->>Pub: publish_run_outcome — snooze suppression → alert_on routing → dedup → publish\n    Note over W,Pub: best-effort — a publish failure never<br/>affects the persisted run\n```\n\nKey sources: [`worker/tasks.py`](../backend/app/worker/tasks.py) (`run_suite`), [`services/run_service.py`](../backend/app/services/run_service.py) (`execute_run`), [`services/run_dispatch.py`](../backend/app/services/run_dispatch.py), [`services/severity.py`](../backend/app/services/severity.py), [`alerting/dispatch.py`](../backend/app/alerting/dispatch.py).\n\n### Orchestration event flow\n\nHow an ADF / Airflow pipeline outcome becomes (at most) a triggered suite run. Everything goes through the `OrchestrationProvider` seam (ADR [0004](adr/0004-orchestration-abstraction.md)) — no provider branching:\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    autonumber\n    participant ORC as ADF / Airflow\n    participant FE as Frontend nginx (public ingress)\n    participant API as FastAPI (internal)\n    participant KV as SecretStore (Key Vault)\n    participant PG as PostgreSQL\n    participant BR as Redis broker\n    participant W as Celery worker\n\n    Note over ORC: pipeline / DAG run reaches a terminal state\n    ORC->>FE: POST /api/v1/orchestration/events/{provider}<br/>(ADF — Azure Monitor alert · Airflow — on_*_callback snippet)\n    FE->>API: same-origin proxy\n    API->>KV: load receiver secret\n    API->>API: authenticate — ADF constant-time token query param (ADR 0006)<br/>· Airflow HMAC-SHA256 over raw body (ADR 0007)\n    API->>API: OrchestrationProvider.parse → RunUpdate or AlertPing\n\n    alt AlertPing — run-anonymous Azure Monitor alert (#492)\n        API->>BR: enqueue targeted poll-now (provider + resource)\n        API-->>ORC: 202 reconciling\n    else RunUpdate\n        API->>PG: upsert pipeline_runs ON (provider, provider_run_id)\n        alt status = succeeded\n            API->>PG: match enabled trigger_bindings (provider, pipeline_or_dag_id, env)\n            API->>PG: INSERT runs, triggered_by = provider:pipeline:run_id<br/>ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING (dedup index, #308)\n            API->>BR: dispatch run_suite per triggered run\n        else status = failed\n            API->>API: alert the user only — failures never trigger suites\n        end\n        API-->>ORC: 200 recorded\n    end\n\n    Note over W,PG: polling fallback (#171) — a 10-min beat sweeps every orchestrator connection<br/>via the provider REST API (list_recent_runs, 15-min lookback) into the SAME<br/>upsert + trigger path; a 30-min gap-recovery sweep (1-hour window) covers downtime\n```\n\nKey sources: [`api/v1/orchestration.py`](../backend/app/api/v1/orchestration.py), [`services/orchestration_service.py`](../backend/app/services/orchestration_service.py), [`worker/tasks.py`](../backend/app/worker/tasks.py) (`poll_orchestration_runs`, `recover_orchestration_gaps`).\n\n## Status semantics\n\n### Run lifecycle\n\n`runs.status` describes **execution, not data quality** — a run whose checks all failed is still `succeeded`.\n\n```mermaid\nstateDiagram-v2\n    [*] --> queued : run created (manual · schedule · trigger binding)\n    queued --> running : worker picks up the task\n    queued --> cancelled : user cancel — task revoked + cooperative start-check\n    queued --> failed : dispatch / setup / target failure — or reaper past threshold (#309)\n    running --> succeeded : execution completed (even when checks FAILED — run status is execution, not data quality)\n    running --> failed : adapter raised — or worker died and the reaper swept it (#309)\n    running --> cancelled : user cancel (DB status set; worker honours cooperatively)\n    succeeded --> [*]\n    failed --> [*]\n    cancelled --> [*]\n```\n\n### Result status derivation\n\n`results.status` has two orthogonal families: the four **severity tiers** (ADR [0005](adr/0005-severity-tier-weights.md)) and the two **operational statuses** (#122). Only the tiers carry health-score weight (0.5 / 1.0 / 2.0 for warn / fail / critical); `skip`/`error` **must be excluded from the health-score denominator**.\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart TD\n    O[\"CheckOutcome (from the runner)\"] --> E{\"runner could evaluate it?\"}\n    E -- \"no — evaluation raised\" --> ERR[\"error — operational (#122)<br/>no tier · no metric · error message in observed_value\"]\n    E -- yes --> S{\"flat-file batch landed?<br/>(decided before execution)\"}\n    S -- no --> SKIP[\"skip — operational (#122)<br/>not evaluated at all\"]\n    S -- yes --> M{\"thresholds set AND a<br/>bandable metric_value exists?\"}\n    M -- \"no — binary fallback (ADR 0005)\" --> BIN{\"GX success?\"}\n    BIN -- yes --> PASS[pass]\n    BIN -- no --> FAIL[fail]\n    M -- yes --> BAND[\"band unexpected-% (0-100, higher = worse)<br/>against warn / fail / critical thresholds (ADR 0016)\"]\n    BAND --> TIER[\"pass / warn / fail / critical<br/>thresholds are policy — they OVERRIDE GX success\"]\n\n    classDef op fill:#F1EFE8,stroke:#5F5E5A,color:#444441\n    classDef tier fill:#E6F1FB,stroke:#185FA5,color:#0C449C\n    class ERR,SKIP op\n    class PASS,FAIL,TIER tier\n```\n\nThe single decision lives in [`services/severity.py`](../backend/app/services/severity.py) (`resolve_status`), shared by run persistence and the check-editor dry-run so a preview can never disagree with the run it previews.\n\n## Trust boundaries & authentication\n\nThe one-diagram consolidation of ADRs [0006](adr/0006-adf-webhook-authentication.md) / [0007](adr/0007-airflow-callback-model.md) / [0008](adr/0008-mcp-server.md) / [0028](adr/0028-cloud-neutral-image-runtime-config-generic-oidc.md) — what crosses each boundary and what credential it carries:\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n    subgraph internet[\"🌍 Untrusted — public internet\"]\n        B[\"Browser (React SPA)\"]\n        AI[\"AI clients (MCP)\"]\n        WH[\"ADF / Airflow webhooks\"]\n    end\n    IDP[\"🔑 OIDC authority<br/>(Azure AD behind the generic DATAQ_AUTH_* contract)\"]\n    subgraph aca[\"🟦 Trust boundary — Container Apps env\"]\n        FE[\"Frontend nginx — SOLE public ingress (TLS)<br/>serves the SPA + runtime auth config (non-secret)\"]\n        API[\"FastAPI — internal ingress only<br/>validates EVERY request itself (no EasyAuth)\"]\n        WK[\"Celery worker — no ingress\"]\n    end\n    subgraph backing[\"🔒 Credentialed backing services\"]\n        KV[\"Key Vault (all connection + webhook secrets)\"]\n        PG[(\"PostgreSQL — dataq db,<br/>least-priv dataq_app role\")]\n        RD[(\"Redis — password auth\")]\n        APPI[\"App Insights (PII-redacted logs + traces)\"]\n    end\n    subgraph egress[\"🌐 Outbound — credentials fetched from Key Vault per use\"]\n        DS[\"Datasources — Snowflake · ADLS · S3 · Unity Catalog\"]\n        AL[\"Teams / Slack webhooks · SMTP\"]\n        OAPI[\"ADF / Airflow REST APIs (polling)\"]\n    end\n\n    B -- \"OIDC auth-code + PKCE\" --> IDP\n    AI -- \"same bearer token\" --> IDP\n    B -- \"HTTPS · bearer JWT on /api\" --> FE\n    AI -- \"bearer JWT on /mcp\" --> FE\n    WH -- \"ADF: shared-secret token in URL (ADR 0006)<br/>Airflow: HMAC-SHA256 body signature (ADR 0007)\" --> FE\n    FE -- \"same-origin proxy /api · /mcp · /healthz (HTTP/1.1)\" --> API\n    API -- \"JWT validated (fastapi-azure-auth / MCP JWTVerifier)<br/>+ per-suite authz + sample redaction\" --> API\n    API -- \"enqueue tasks\" --> RD\n    RD -- \"deliver tasks\" --> WK\n    API -- \"UAMI — no stored credential\" --> KV\n    WK -- \"UAMI — no stored credential\" --> KV\n    API --> PG\n    WK --> PG\n    API --> APPI\n    WK --> APPI\n    WK -- \"GX checks / monitor SQL\" --> DS\n    WK -- \"run-outcome alerts\" --> AL\n    WK -- \"10-min poll fallback\" --> OAPI\n\n    classDef hub fill:#E6F1FB,stroke:#185FA5,color:#0C449C\n    classDef ext fill:#F1EFE8,stroke:#5F5E5A,color:#444441\n    classDef sec fill:#FAEEDA,stroke:#854F0B,color:#633806\n    classDef out fill:#E1F5EE,stroke:#0F6E56,color:#085041\n    class FE,API,WK hub\n    class B,AI,WH,IDP ext\n    class KV,PG,RD,APPI sec\n    class DS,AL,OAPI out\n    style internet fill:#F7F6F2,stroke:#5F5E5A\n    style aca fill:#F4F9FE,stroke:#185FA5\n    style backing fill:#FDF7EC,stroke:#854F0B\n    style egress fill:#F0FBF7,stroke:#0F6E56\n```\n\nBoundary notes:\n\n- **Defense in depth, not perimeter trust:** the API validates every request's bearer JWT itself (`fastapi-azure-auth` for REST, `JWTVerifier` for MCP — same tenant/audience/scope) even though it is only reachable through the frontend proxy. Platform-level auth (SWA EasyAuth) is explicitly disabled (#511).\n- **The only endpoints that bypass user JWT auth** are the two orchestration webhook receivers (each with its own secret scheme, above) and the health probe. Webhook secrets live in Key Vault and are compared constant-time; they are never logged.\n- **Nothing secret is baked into images or served to the browser.** The frontend's runtime `DATAQ_AUTH_*` config is non-secret OIDC metadata (ADR 0028); all real secrets resolve at use-time from Key Vault via user-assigned managed identity.\n- **MCP is fail-closed:** without resolvable auth config the `/mcp` mount does not come up at all (ADR 0008).\n\n## Key invariants"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/tests/db/test_architecture_er_sync.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Drift guard: the ER diagram in docs/architecture.md tracks the real schema.\n\nThe diagram is hand-maintained (docs rule: update it in the same PR as any\nmodel/migration change), so this pins the *table-level* contract both ways —\na model table missing from the diagram, or a diagram entity naming a table\nthat no longer exists, fails CI. Column-level sync stays a review concern\n(checking every attribute here would just duplicate the models file).\n\nPure-unit: reads the markdown + `Base.metadata`; no DB.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom backend.app.db import models\nfrom backend.app.db.base import Base\n\n_REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]\n_ARCHITECTURE_MD = _REPO_ROOT / \"docs\" / \"architecture.md\"\n\n# An entity definition inside the erDiagram block: `    users {`\n_ENTITY_RE = re.compile(r\"^\\s*(\\w+)\\s*\\{\", re.MULTILINE)\n_ER_BLOCK_RE = re.compile(r\"```mermaid\\s*\\nerDiagram\\n(.*?)```\", re.DOTALL)\n\n\ndef _er_diagram_entities() -> set[str]:\n    match = _ER_BLOCK_RE.search(_ARCHITECTURE_MD.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    assert match, \"docs/architecture.md has no ```mermaid erDiagram``` block\"\n    return set(_ENTITY_RE.findall(match.group(1)))\n\n\ndef test_models_module_exports_every_mapped_table() -> None:\n    # Sanity for the guard itself: the metadata the tests compare against is\n    # populated by importing the models module.\n    assert models.Base is Base\n    assert Base.metadata.tables, \"Base.metadata is empty — models not registered\"\n\n\ndef test_every_table_appears_in_the_er_diagram() -> None:\n    missing = set(Base.metadata.tables) - _er_diagram_entities()\n    assert not missing, (\n        f\"tables missing from the docs/architecture.md ER diagram: {sorted(missing)} — \"\n        \"update the diagram in the same PR as the model/migration change\"\n    )\n\n\ndef test_er_diagram_has_no_stale_tables() -> None:\n    stale = _er_diagram_entities() - set(Base.metadata.tables)\n    assert not stale, (\n        f\"docs/architecture.md ER diagram names tables that no longer exist: {sorted(stale)} — \"\n        \"remove them from the diagram\"\n    )\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/CHANGELOG.md",
  "content": "# Changelog\n\nAll notable user-facing changes to DataQ are documented here. The format follows\n[Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) and the project uses\n[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/).\n\n**Cadence:** one curated entry per **minor/major release, written at tag time** —\nnot per PR. Patch releases get an entry only when the change is user-visible or\nsecurity-relevant. Raw material comes from the squashed conventional commits\n(`git log v<prev>..HEAD --oneline`); curate it down to what a deployer or user\nwould act on. The step lives in the release checklist in\n[docs/runbook-faq.md](docs/runbook-faq.md).\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n## [1.0.0] — 2026-07-04\n\nFirst production release. Single-tenant data quality monitoring platform on\nGreat Expectations (GX Core), deployed to Azure Container Apps.\n\n### Added\n\n- **Datasources (4):** Snowflake (DEV/QA/UAT), ADLS Gen2, AWS S3, and Unity\n  Catalog (Databricks) behind a common `ConnectionAdapter`/`CheckRunner` seam,\n  with a connection manager UI, connection testing, re-auth, and per-connection\n  version history. All credentials live in Azure Key Vault — never in the DB.\n- **Checks & suites:** catalog-driven GX expectation editor + Monaco custom-SQL\n  checks (read-only, single-statement guardrails — ADR 0019); freshness & volume\n  monitor kinds (ADR 0012); severity tiers `warn`/`fail`/`critical` derived from\n  user thresholds banding the GX unexpected-% (ADR 0005/0016); dry-run preview;\n  column profiler; per-check version history; suite export/import.\n- **Execution:** async Celery runs across all datasource types with live\n  per-check progress, cancel, flat-file batch resolution (absent batch → skip,\n  not failure), a stuck-run reaper, and cron scheduling (DST-aware, no-backfill).\n- **Orchestration (monitor + trigger only, never datasources):** ADF and Apache\n  Airflow behind one `OrchestrationProvider` interface — webhook receivers\n  (shared-secret / HMAC-signed), a 10-minute polling fallback, downtime gap\n  recovery, and trigger bindings that start suite runs on pipeline success.\n- **Results & dashboard:** in-app Results page with run drill-down,\n  observed-vs-expected, PII-redacted failing-row samples (column-aware policy +\n  retention purge), pipeline-runs correlation, and a monitoring dashboard with\n  health score, pass-rate, and trends.\n- **Alerting:** MS Teams, Slack, and email publishers behind the\n  `ResultPublisher` seam with severity-aware routing, dedup, per-check snooze,\n  and per-suite delivery config.\n- **Access model:** Azure AD SSO via a generic OIDC contract (`DATAQ_AUTH_*`,\n  no MSAL — ADR 0028); suite-level sharing (`owner`/`edit`/`view`); implicit\n  workspace-admin (ADR 0027) with an admin control centre.\n- **AI integration:** 8 curated FastMCP tools at `/mcp`, validated with the same\n  Azure AD bearer token, fail-closed without auth (ADR 0008).\n- **Deployment:** in-repo Terraform for Azure Container Apps (API internal,\n  frontend the sole public surface), GHCR multi-arch images, GitHub Actions\n  build→migrate→deploy pipeline, App Insights logs + OpenTelemetry traces.\n\n[Unreleased]: https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/compare/v1.0.0...HEAD\n[1.0.0]: https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/releases/tag/v1.0.0\n"
}
